=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two positive integers n and k.
An integer x is called k-palindromic if:

x is a palindrome.
x is divisible by k.

Return the largest integer having n digits (as a string) that is k-palindromic.
Note that the integer must not have leading zeros.
 
Example 1:

Input: n = 3, k = 5
Output: "595"
Explanation:
595 is the largest k-palindromic integer with 3 digits.

Example 2:

Input: n = 1, k = 4
Output: "8"
Explanation:
4 and 8 are the only k-palindromic integers with 1 digit.

Example 3:

Input: n = 5, k = 6
Output: "89898"

 
Constraints:

1 <= n <= 10^5
1 <= k <= 9

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def largestPalindrome(self, n: int, k: int) -> str:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:

class Solution:
    def largestPalindrome(self, n: int, k: int) -> str:
        m = (n + 1)//2
        # precompute pow10 mod k for t=0..n-1
        pow10 = [1]*n
        for t in range(1,n):
            pow10[t] = (pow10[t-1]*10) % k
        coeff = [0]*m
        for i in range(m):
            j = n-1-i
            if i == j:
                coeff[i] = pow10[i] % k
            else:
                coeff[i] = (pow10[i] + pow10[j]) % k

        # precompute add_shift for masks up to 1<<k
        size = 1 << k
        add_shift = [[0]*k for _ in range(size)]? Actually shift amount s 0..k-1. For each mask, s, result.
        for mask in range(size):
            for s in range(k):
                res=0
                mm=mask
                while mm:
                    lsb = mm & -mm
                    r = lsb.bit_length()-1
                    res |= 1 << ((r+s)%k)
                    mm -= lsb
                add_shift[mask][s] = res
        # Could optimize by precomputing for each mask and shift using bit operations? k small.

        suffix = [0]*(m+1)
        suffix[m] = 1  # bit 0
        for i in range(m-1, 0, -1):
            c = coeff[i]
            mask=0
            # digits 0..9
            # To avoid repeated shift lookup? 
            for d in range(10):
                s = (d*c) % k
                mask |= add_shift[suffix[i+1]][s]
            suffix[i] = mask

        # Greedy
        res_chars=[]
        cur=0
        for i in range(m):
            c = coeff[i]
            # choose descending
            start = 9 if i>0 else 9? Actually first digit cannot be 0, so descending from 9 to 1. If i==0 range(9,0,-1), else range(9,-1,-1).
            for d in range(9, 0, -1) if i==0 else range(9, -1, -1):
                need = (- (cur + d*c)) % k
                if (suffix[i+1] >> need) & 1:
                    res_chars.append(str(d))
                    cur = (cur + d*c) % k
                    break
        return ''.join(res_chars)

Let's scrutinize DP and greedy in detail.

We have positions in first half: 0..m-1. Let full palindrome digits P[0..n-1] with P[i]=a_i for i<m and P[n-1-i]=a_i. For each i, contribution to number modulo k is a_i * (10^i + 10^{n-1-i}) mod k if i != n-1-i, else a_i * 10^i. Let coeff[i] = that. Then total mod k = sum_i a_i * coeff[i] mod k. This is linear. We want choose a_i to make sum 0 mod k.

Suffix DP: suffix[t] = set of residues achievable by assigning a_i for i=t..m-1. For t=m, empty assignment residue 0. For t>=1, recurrence:
suffix[t] = { (d*coeff[t] + r) mod k | d in 0..9, r in suffix[t+1] }.
We compute for t=m-1..1. We don't compute t=0 because first digit has restricted d=1..9 and not needed for greedy? Actually if we wanted suffix[0] maybe restricted. But for greedy at t=0, we need suffix[1] (completions after first digit). For t>0, suffix[t] includes positions t..end. Good.

Greedy:
cur = sum chosen a_i * coeff[i] mod k.
At position t, for candidate d, after choosing d, remaining positions t+1..end must supply residue need = -(cur + d*coeff[t]) mod k. This is possible iff need in suffix[t+1]. We choose largest d satisfying. Since suffix[t+1] exactly set of possible residues, greedy yields lexicographically largest sequence.

Need check for t=m-1: suffix[m]={0}; condition need=0. Good.

Now, one subtlety: For n odd, center position i=m-1 has coeff[i]=10^i mod k. But in suffix DP for t=m-1, d ranges 0..9. Good. For t=0 if n=1, coeff[0]=10^0=1. Greedy uses suffix[1]={0}. Good.

Another subtlety: The coefficient for paired positions if i != j. But for n even, i ranges 0..m-1, j=n-1-i ranges m..n-1, no overlap. For n odd, i=m-1 center. Good.

Need maybe compute pow10 up to n-1. For n=1, pow10 length1. Good.

Greedy need = (-(cur + d*0)) %1 =0. suffix[i+1] >> 0 &1. suffix[i+1] is 1. Good. cur = (cur+0)%1 =0. Output all 9. Good.

Let's optimize code for clarity and speed.

Implementation details:

```
class Solution:
    def largestPalindrome(self, n: int, k: int) -> str:
        m = (n + 1) // 2
        
        pow10 = [1] * n
        for i in range(1, n):
            pow10[i] = (pow10[i-1] * 10) % k
        
        coeff = [0] * m
        for i in range(m):
            j = n - 1 - i
            if i == j:
                coeff[i] = pow10[i]
            else:
                coeff[i] = (pow10[i] + pow10[j]) % k
```

If k=1, pow10 values all 0, coeff all 0. Fine.

Precompute add_shift:

```
        size = 1 << k
        add_shift = [[0] * k for _ in range(size)]
        for mask in range(size):
            for s in range(k):
                res = 0
                mm = mask
                while mm:
                    lsb = mm & -mm
                    r = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                    res |= 1 << ((r + s) % k)
                    mm -= lsb
                add_shift[mask][s] = res
```

Could optimize by precomputing bits list for each mask:
```
        bits = [[] for _ in range(size)]
        for mask in range(size):
            for r in range(k):
                if mask >> r & 1:
                    bits[mask].append(r)
        add_shift = [[0]*k for _ in range(size)]
        for mask in range(size):
            for s in range(k):
                res=0
                for r in bits[mask]:
                    res |= 1 << ((r+s)%k)
                add_shift[mask][s]=res
```
Either fine. k small. `bit_length` okay.

Precompute trans:

```
        trans = [[0] * size for _ in range(k)]
        for c in range(k):
            for mask in range(size):
                res = 0
                for d in range(10):
                    s = (d * c) % k
                    res |= add_shift[mask][s]
                trans[c][mask] = res
```

But note if k=1, size=2, c range(1), s=0. Good.

Compute suffix:

```
        suffix = [0] * (m + 1)
        suffix[m] = 1
        for i in range(m - 1, 0, -1):
            suffix[i] = trans[coeff[i]][suffix[i+1]]
```

Wait, is `trans[coeff[i]][suffix[i+1]]` correct? trans[c][mask] = OR over d of add_shift[mask][(d*c)%k] = set of residues achievable by choosing digit d at current position and then any residue in mask from suffix. Yes.

But note trans includes all digits 0..9. For suffix starts i>=1, yes. For i=m-1, suffix[m]=1. Good.

Now greedy:

```
        ans = []
        cur = 0
        for i in range(m):
            c = coeff[i]
            if i == 0:
                digits = range(9, 0, -1)
            else:
                digits = range(9, -1, -1)
            for d in digits:
                need = (- (cur + d * c)) % k
                if (suffix[i+1] >> need) & 1:
                    ans.append(str(d))
                    cur = (cur + d * c) % k
                    break
        return ''.join(ans)
```

Need check if `suffix[i+1]` for i=0 is suffix[1], computed. For m=1, suffix[1]=suffix[m]=1. Good.

Potential issue: For i=0, if m=1, suffix[1]=1. For i=0, `digits = range(9,0,-1)`. Good.

Potential issue: For i>0, digits includes 0. If no digit found, ans length less; but should not. Could add assertion? Not needed.

Potential final code:

```python
class Solution:
    def largestPalindrome(self, n: int, k: int) -> str:
        m = (n + 1) // 2

        pow10 = [1 % k] * n
        for i in range(1, n):
            pow10[i] = (pow10[i - 1] * 10) % k

        coeff = [0] * m
        for i in range(m):
            j = n - 1 - i
            if i == j:
                coeff[i] = pow10[i]
            else:
                coeff[i] = (pow10[i] + pow10[j]) % k

        size = 1 << k
        add_shift = [[0] * k for _ in range(size)]
        for mask in range(size):
            for s in range(k):
                res = 0
                mm = mask
                while mm:
                    lsb = mm & -mm
                    r = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                    res |= 1 << ((r + s) % k)
                    mm -= lsb
                add_shift[mask][s] = res

        trans = [[0] * size for _ in range(k)]
        for c in range(k):
            for mask in range(size):
                res = 0
                for d in range(10):
                    res |= add_shift[mask][(d * c) % k]
                trans[c][mask] = res

        suffix = [0] * (m + 1)
        suffix[m] = 1
        for i in range(m - 1, 0, -1):
            suffix[i] = trans[coeff[i]][suffix[i + 1]]

        first_chars = []
        cur = 0
        for i in range(m):
            c = coeff[i]
            if i == 0:
                digit_range = range(9, 0, -1)
            else:
                digit_range = range(9, -1, -1)
            for d in digit_range:
                need = (-(cur + d * c)) % k
                if (suffix[i + 1] >> need) & 1:
                    first_chars.append(str(d))
                    cur = (cur + d * c) % k
                    break

        first = ''.join(first_chars)
        if n % 2 == 0:
            return first + first[::-1]
        else:
            return first + first[:-1][::-1]
```

Need check `pow10 = [1 % k] * n` for k=1: all 0. For k>1: all 1 initially, loop sets. Good.

Potential issue: For k=1, `size = 1 << 1 = 2`, `add_shift` list size 2, but masks only 0 or1. `trans` size 2. Good.

Potential issue: For k=1, `trans` precompute `for c in range(k)` range(1), c=0. `for d in range(10): res |= add_shift[mask][(d*0)%1]` = add_shift[mask][0]. add_shift[1][0] =1. Good.

Potential issue: For k=1, `suffix[i] = trans[0][suffix[i+1]]`. suffix[m]=1. trans[0][1]=1. Good.

Potential issue: For k=1, `need = (-(cur + d*0)) % 1` =0. `(suffix[i+1] >> 0) & 1` =1. Good.

Now, let's think about if
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def largestPalindrome(self, n: int, k: int) -> str:
        m = (n + 1) // 2

        pow10 = [1 % k] * n
        for i in range(1, n):
            pow10[i] = (pow10[i - 1] * 10) % k

        coeff = [0] * m
        for i in range(m):
            j = n - 1 - i
            if i == j:
                coeff[i] = pow10[i]
            else:
                coeff[i] = (pow10[i] + pow10[j]) % k

        size = 1 << k
        add_shift = [[0] * k for _ in range(size)]
        for mask in range(size):
            for s in range(k):
                res = 0
                mm = mask
                while mm:
                    lsb = mm & -mm
                    r = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                    res |= 1 << ((r + s) % k)
                    mm -= lsb
                add_shift[mask][s] = res

        trans = [[0] * size for _ in range(k)]
        for c in range(k):
            for mask in range(size):
                res = 0
                for d in range(10):
                    res |= add_shift[mask][(d * c) % k]
                trans[c][mask] = res

        suffix = [0] * (m + 1)
        suffix[m] = 1  # empty suffix has residue 0

        for i in range(m - 1, 0, -1):
            suffix[i] = trans[coeff[i]][suffix[i + 1]]

        first_half = []
        cur = 0

        for i in range(m):
            c = coeff[i]
            digits = range(9, 0, -1) if i == 0 else range(9, -1, -1)

            for d in digits:
                need = (-(cur + d * c)) % k
                if (suffix[i + 1] >> need) & 1:
                    first_half.append(str(d))
                    cur = (cur + d * c) % k
                    break

        first = ''.join(first_half)

        if n % 2 == 0:
            return first + first[::-1]
        else:
            return first + first[:-1][::-1]
```
